Iteration 49/50 — Inference/Training Pipeline UI, Compute Routing, Mesh Status, and ML Excretion/Absorption

49.1. Real-Time Inference and Training Pipeline UI (User-Facing & Admin)
A. Visualizing the Lifecycle of an AI Build
1. “Pipeline Builder” Main Interface

Graph-Based UI:

Users create pipelines as node graphs (think: TensorBoard, but actionable).

Nodes: Data source (local/global), preprocessing, augmentation, model arch, hyperparams, training engine, evaluation, deployment.

Drag-and-Drop:

Each stage is added by dragging from a menu; connections are color-coded:

Blue = data flow, Yellow = transformation, Red = error/rollback, Green = deployment/inference.

Preset Templates:

1-click to “Train Chatbot,” “Image Classifier,” “Game AI NPC,” etc., auto-populating nodes with best defaults for novices.

2. Cross-Session Memory/Job Management

Auto-Save Drafts:

Every change is versioned; rollback/compare at any step.

Job Queue:

UI shows all in-progress, completed, scheduled, and failed jobs per user and globally (for admins/absolute users).

AI Feedback Loop:

The system recommends improvements in real time (“Consider more data for task X,” “Hyperparameter Y out of optimal range”).

3. Local-Global Execution Toggle

Single Switch:

Users choose “Train locally” (their machine), “Train globally” (crowdsourced mesh), or “Hybrid” (uses local for privacy-sensitive tasks, global for heavy lifting).

Cost/ETA Calculator:

As users build, a sidebar shows estimated time, compute required, energy credits, and cost if using global resources.

B. Compute Routing, Donation, and Allocation (AIOS Mesh Layer)
1. Node Discovery and Registration

Peer Discovery:

Auto-detects local and LAN nodes via mDNS, then seeks global mesh via decentralized registry (inspired by BitTorrent tracker/DHT with extra layers for security/privacy).

Resource Declaration:

Each node declares: CPU, GPU, RAM, bandwidth, storage, privacy settings, allowed file paths/folders, time available.

2. Secure Job Scheduling

Global Scheduler:

Smart scheduling algorithm splits jobs by hardware, data privacy, latency, and trust ranking (user can set compute trust to “global,” “friends,” “my LAN,” or “me only”).

Job Chunking:

Large ML jobs are sharded into minimal chunks; each chunk hashed, signed, and routed only to eligible nodes.

Encryption & Sandboxing:

All jobs run in signed containers (Docker/similar), with zero-access to user files not permitted for the task. On job finish, logs are wiped, results excreted.

3. Real-Time Status and Control Panel

Mesh Visualizer:

See all connected nodes—local, LAN, global—rendered as animated, color-coded orbs (size = compute, color = current workload, glow = reliability/uptime score).

Live Job List:

Clicking a node shows what it’s working on, history, reliability score, and “trust” settings.

Pause/Kill/Isolate:

Users (and absolute admins) can halt, re-assign, or isolate misbehaving jobs/nodes with a click.

C. ML Excretion, Absorption, and Dataset Logging
1. Real-Time Excretion (Code/Data Output)

Every AI/ML/Gameplay Event Generates an “Excretion File”:

These are structured logs (JSON, Parquet, or binary format) recording all actions, results, mistakes, edge cases, game strategies, and social behaviors.

E.g., “player_A used tactic_X, enemy_B responded with Y, result: win/loss, game state: …”

Public or Private Logging:

Players and creators choose: excretions sent to local sandbox, global pool, or private archive.

Game & Social Logs Used for Training:

Logs feed into the organism’s CAE/AE/ICAE cycles, becoming both the “memory” and the training set for all future models—including NPCs, chatbots, social AI, and even QOL/game update models.

2. Absorption (Model/Data Intake)

New Excretions Are Scanned:

The mesh automatically classifies and indexes every log by type, relevance, and cross-references against existing datasets for novelty, error, or QOL feedback.

Automated Model Retraining:

When enough new excretions are collected, the system triggers partial retraining for affected models (e.g., “NPC_boss behaviors improved based on last 1,000 player strategies”).

Mutation/Version Control:

Every change to a model or data pipeline is checkpointed and can be reverted, branched, or forked (public/private), allowing “sandboxed” evolution and merging back into the main organism by admin/consensus.

D. UX for Excretion/Absorption/Job Control
Player/Gamer:

See “What You’ve Taught the Organism” — a timeline or mind map showing how your game tactics and social choices changed the world or AI’s next-gen abilities.

AI Builder:

“Excretion Browser” lets users/teams inspect, curate, and re-weight logs before absorption, optionally annotating or blacklisting problematic data.

Absolute Users/Admin:

“Excretion Fusion” dashboard for merging, splitting, and diff’ing excreted data/model shards globally.

E. The Organism’s Continuous Improvement Cycle
All User and System Excretions → Indexed, Absorbed, Mutated, and Used to Drive the Fractal Growth of Games, Social Platform, and the AIOS IO Organism Itself.

Every pipeline stage, model, and data point remains explainable and auditable.

Iteration 52/50 — Fractal System Deep-Dive: GeoRPG’s Interlocked Modes, Fractal Currencies, Organism Integration, and Full UX

A. GeoRPG — Fractal Game Chain Structure and Interplay
1. Unlocks and Progression
Entry:

Unlocked when the player has generated 100 main GeoBIT world maps. A singularity/“fractal resonance” animation signals unlock, with a new portal appearing in the hub.

All relevant stats (levels, abilities, currency balances) from 9Pixel and GeoBIT are displayed in a cross-fractal stats panel with color-coded forward (blue) and reverse (green) influence percentages.

2. Core Game Modes in GeoRPG
a. Tower Defense (TD) Mode

Mechanics:

Players set up tower defenses in PvE or PvP scenarios.

Currencies: Vapor (instant battle upgrades, max 10 on hand), Particles (major upgrades, instant use), Fluid (only PvE, speeds research).

Resource Excretion: Every round, a detailed combat log (moves, build order, outcome) is excreted and sent to the AI organism for learning.

Progression:

Reach wave 50 in TD to unlock GeoRPG RPG Mode.

Every 100 tower levels, player receives cross-mode stat bonuses (e.g., +0.1% main attack for GeoRPG RPG, XP boosts for GeoBIT, etc.).

UX:

Visual health border around the screen (red for enemy HP, green for player/towers), fractal overlays show cross-mode boosts and fractal progress.

Tooltip overlays explain how current stats/currencies influence or are influenced by other fractal games.

b. MMORPG Mode

Mechanics:

Play as a “mobile tower” in a live world; anchor in PvP or PvE zones for defense/offense buffs.

“Anchor” mechanic: anchoring spawns troops, buffs defense, logs excretion of all player/AI/NPC moves during defense for LLM training.

Loot drop and bag mechanics as described in canon.

Unlock:

RPG Mode unlocks upon reaching TD wave 50.

UX:

World map shows live player positions, node health, and resource flows (all backed by real compute donation stats).

Anchoring triggers a fractal visualization of defensive/excretory links between modes and global organism.

c. Base Builder Mode

Mechanics:

City/base building, research, resource management.

Fluid speeds research (PvE only), Vapor/Particle cross-mode transfers with hard taxes (90% loss forward, 89.9999999% gain reverse, as previously described).

Building upgrades and research logs are excreted for AI organism analysis.

Unlock:

Base Builder unlocks by accumulating and spending in-game currency.

UX:

Drag-and-drop building interface, modern neon grid layout, with info overlays showing how builder choices echo into TD/MMORPG and GoL (bi-directional fractality).

Visual queues show when cross-fractal resource flows occur, e.g., a “fractal pulse” effect animates during major resource transfers.

d. Game of Life (GoL)

Mechanics:

Biological single-cell “tower” play, underwater movement, omnidirectional spawning, focus on “Mutation” as prestige.

Currencies: Protein (in-battle only, lost if unused), Genetic Material (GM, for meta upgrades, lost if not collected), Particles/Vapor (for upgrades, taxed on transfer).

Level 100 cap, ~10 years no-lifer path, entirely skill/gated.

Account-wide green stat overlays for Mutation, visible on every stat panel throughout all fractal games (but never used for color coding outside stats).

Unlock:

GoL unlocks via hidden pattern, rare in-game achievement, or secret event.

UX:

Whirlpool and movement effects, all particle/GM/protein drops visualized as floating geometric orbs.

Progress toward GoL and mutation overlays are shown in green, with separate stat tab dedicated to GoL achievements.

e. OrbsDungeon

Mechanics:

Unlocked after GoL wave 100.

Fractalizes all previous mechanics, incorporates every resource, enemy, and upgrade type into a hyper-complex, procedurally-generated dungeon world with fractal world layers, bosses, and loot.

Excretion logs here are the richest—encompass battle data, build paths, chat, economic choices, and every fractalized upgrade for organism learning.

UX:

Hyper-dense, glowing dungeon interface, fractal overlays showing cross-game effects, progression, and world fractality.

Live event feed displays how OrbsDungeon activity ripples “upwards” through the entire platform (AI organism, social system, global stats).

B. Fractal Currencies & Cross-Game Influence
Currencies:

Vapor: Always capped at 10 for real-time strategy use; heavily taxed on transfer between games to prevent farming abuse.

Particles: Universal upgrade, no cap; serves as the main account-wide progression metric and used for major research/upgrade unlocks.

Fluid: Premium currency (never PvP), speeds non-combat upgrades, can only be earned (not bought), used to advance long-timer research and meta-progression.

Genetic Material & Protein: Unique to GoL and OrbsDungeon, account-bound and tracked in global stat overlays.

UI Implementation:

Currencies are always visible in the global top-bar, with color-coded fractal arrows to show the direction of effect and gain/loss in each mode.

Tooltips and info overlays describe exactly how every action in one game will affect others, with hover events and real-time update animations.

New users start in 9Pixel; UI explains fractal game structure and future unlocks as “singularity pulses.”

Users can opt-in to let their gameplay, social interactions, and build preferences be used to train their AI twin or the global organism, visualized in a “data donation” dashboard.

Users can merge their gameplay data with social presence to build ever-smarter AI personalities, and can compete, collaborate, or form clusters for group rewards and AI model training sessions.

Dataset Selection
